# Medplum capability inventory

> What Medplum's server actually does, row by row, and what Big Book does about each row. Source-first: the rows come from `medplum/medplum` code, the docs supply the "why" link, the examples supply the "who needs it" column. Companion to `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` (which says what Big Book commits to) and `docs/guides/medplum-parity.md` (observed status codes).
> Owner: Claude Code drafts area by area; Tanvir blesses the decision list per area; REQUIREMENTS.md is reconciled after each bless. If a row here and a brick disagree, the brick is wrong until it is edited.

Snapshot: `medplum/medplum` `main` @ `fbc8e7b4b` (2026-09-11, server 5.1.x); big-book @ `3ec80a8` (ADR-003 and ADR-004 decided); docs scrape 2026-09-12 (`docs/medplum-docs/`, git-ignored; regenerate with `tools/scrape_medplum_docs.py`).

## Conventions

- **Row schema**: `ID | Capability | Source (file:line) | Behaviour | Doc ref (path — menu breadcrumb) | Used by | Proposed fate | BB-R`.
- **Fate**: `wire HAPI` / `wire Keycloak` (upstream does it; expose + configure), `glue` (Big Book code, counts against the 5–10k budget), `defer v0.x` (named version), `non-goal` (with reason).
- **BB-R**: the brick in REQUIREMENTS.md that owns the row; `GAP` = no brick mentions it.
- **Source paths** are relative to `packages/server/src` unless stated; `client.ts` = `packages/core/src/client.ts`.
- **Status per area**: `draft` (agents produced, Claude merged) → `blessed` (Tanvir approved the decision list) → `reconciled` (REQUIREMENTS.md updated).

| Area | Scope | Rows | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Unique layer: admin routes, custom resource types, auth/OAuth | 46 + 31 + ~190 element rows + 74 | draft |
| 2 | FHIR routes and repository behaviour (`fhir/`) | 71 + 72 + 100 | draft |
| 3 | FHIR operations (85 across `fhir/operations`) | 40 + 45 | draft |
| 4 | Project settings, features, server config keys | — | pending |
| 5 | `MedplumClient` public surface (`packages/core`) | — | pending |
| 6 | Workers: subscriptions, cron, bots, bulk, reindex | — | pending |

---

## Area 1 — decisions needed

Deduplicated from the three sub-sections below. **D** = design call (changes a brick or an ADR line), **T** = text fix (brick wording is wrong about Medplum; no design change). Each carries a recommendation; a single "bless" applies all recommendations, or answer by number.

### Design calls

| # | Topic | What Medplum does | What the docs say now | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | `X-Project` header (ADR-004 #4, BB-R-005.7, BB-R-013.1) | No such header. Super-admin targets another project only via the `:projectId` path on `invite` and `client`; on every other admin route the path id is ignored and the token's project is used; cross-project FHIR writes set `meta.project` in extended mode. `@medplum/core` never sends `X-Project`. | "a super-admin write without it gets 400" | Keep `X-Project` as a Big Book extension: **optional, never required**. Path id wins when present; without either, act on the token's own project (Medplum behaviour). Drop the 400 rule from ADR-004 #4 / issue #4. |
| D2 | Admin routes that do not exist in Medplum (`GET /admin/projects`, `PUT /admin/projects/:id`, `GET …/members`, `PUT …/members/:mid`; ADR-004 screens, BB-R-014.4) | Project list = `/auth/me` + FHIR `Project` search; project edit = `POST …/settings|secrets|sites` (whole-array replace); members = FHIR `ProjectMembership?profile-type=`; member update = `POST …/members/:mid` (full resource, 403 on shape error); also `POST …/bot`, `POST …/setpassword`, `…/members/:mid/mfa/reset|resetpassword`. | BB-R-014.4 lists the invented spellings as the compat surface | Keep the four BB routes for Appsmith in v0.1, labelled **BB-only**. Rewrite BB-R-014.4 with Medplum's real spellings (ADM-005…027) as the v0.2 compat list; `invite` is the only route `@medplum/core` types (SDK-grade needs only that one byte-exact). |
| D3 | `/oauth2/token` "proxied unchanged" (BB-R-004.8, ADR-003) | SDK reads `project` + `profile` from the **token response body**, and requires `exp`, `client_id` (or `cid`), and `login_id` **claims**; without `login_id` it never calls `/auth/me` and treats the user as signed out. Token carries `profile`, not `project`/`membership`. | §8 says token proxied unchanged; §9 says claims `project`, `profile`, `membership` | v0.1 (curl-level): proxy unchanged, as decided. v0.2 (SDK-grade): **wrap** `/oauth2/token` to add `project` + `profile` to the body; Keycloak mappers for `login_id` (← `sid`), `client_id` (← `azp` on user tokens), `profile`. Reword §8/§9 accordingly; add to BB-R-014.3 exit list. |
| D4 | Other hidden SDK-grade dependencies (BB-R-014.3) | `X-Medplum: extended` on every SDK call ⇒ responses must carry `meta.project`, `meta.author`, `meta.compartment`; `/auth/me` must return `config` with a non-empty `menu` and a **compiled** `accessPolicy`; `ClientApplication.id` must equal the OAuth `client_id`; `Prefer: respond-async` callers expect an `AsyncJob` body at `Content-Location`; `OperationOutcome.id` slugs (already in medplum-parity.md). | Not listed | Add all five to BB-R-014.3 as v0.2 glue with one exit test each. `meta.*` = response interceptor (~100 lines); default-menu generator copied from `auth/me.ts:135-171`. |
| D5 | ClientApplication secret (BB-R-013.3 "shows secret once") | Secret is stored in clear and returned on **every** read of the resource and never masked; `@medplum/app` `ClientsPage` reads it from the resource. Keycloak also lets admins read it. | "create → shows secret once" | Match Medplum: secret retrievable by project admins via the resource read. Drop "once". |
| D6 | Admin-initiated credential routes (ADM-005/026/027/033: force set password, MFA reset, admin reset link) | Exist, called by the Medplum app's Account Security panel; not login flows but admin-API operations. | Not in any brick (GAP) | Add to BB-R-005 as v0.2 `wire Keycloak` (admin API `reset-password`, delete `otp` credential, `execute-actions-email`), routes byte-shaped only at app-grade. |
| D7 | Email-OTP MFA and project-wide `mfaRequired` (BB-R-004.3) | Two factors: TOTP and **email code**; `isMfaRequired = Project.setting[mfaRequired] OR User.mfaRequired`; MFA is skipped for Google/external/exchange logins. | TOTP only, invite flag only | Email OTP → **non-goal v0.x** (Keycloak has no native email OTP; extension only). Project-wide `mfaRequired` → `wire Keycloak` conditional-OTP flow per organisation, v0.2. State both in §3. |
| D8 | Super-admin DB/ops routes (12 rows: reindex, purge, migrations, vacuum, index rebuild, table settings, lambda cleaner, rebuildprojectid, schema drift, setdataversion) | Medplum-schema-specific DDL through HTTP. | Not mentioned | **non-goal** as routes; `reindex` → HAPI `$reindex`, `purge` → HAPI `$expunge` (v0.2 with audit), the rest → `psql`/Flyway runbooks under BB-R-011.7. |
| D9 | External-to-internal token exchange (BB-R-004.2 "token exchange — Keycloak") | Medplum exchanges a third-party access token for a Medplum token by calling the IdP's userinfo. | Assumed native | In Keycloak 26 external-to-internal exchange is a **preview** feature; standard exchange is internal-only. Decide in BB-R-024: enable the preview flag, or glue (~150 lines). Recommend preview flag behind `full` profile, glue only if a pilot needs it. |
| D10 | `medplum` CLI compatibility (BB-R-017) | CLI logs in via a built-in public client `medplum-cli` (redirect `http://localhost:9615`, PKCE optional) and uses **HTTP Basic client credentials on every FHIR call**, not a bearer token. | Not mentioned | v0.2: bootstrap a `medplum-cli` public client in the realm; add a Basic-auth→cached-token filter (~80 lines) or declare Basic-on-resources non-goal and require `medplum login`. Recommend the filter; it is also what `MedplumClient.setBasicAuth` needs. |

### Text fixes (apply on bless, no design change)

| # | Brick | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| T1 | BB-R-006.2 "`admin: true` bypasses" | False. Only `Project.superAdmin` bypasses. `membership.admin` = the member's own policy **plus** a fixed injected rule set for the project-admin types (Project, ProjectMembership, User, Cron, Package*, UserSecurityRequest) with hidden `superAdmin/systemSecret/strictMode` on Project and hidden `passwordHash/mfaSecret` on User. A membership with **no** policy compiles to `{resourceType:'*'}` = full project access. `*` never covers the project-admin types. |
| T2 | BB-R-006.2 "`ProjectMembership.accessPolicy[]`" | `accessPolicy` is `0..1`; the list is `access[]{policy, parameter[]}`; all entries concatenate and OR. |
| T3 | BB-R-006.3 "`%requestor`" | Does not exist. Built-ins are `%profile` and `%patient` (defaults to profile); everything else is `access[].parameter`. Substitution is textual over the policy JSON (`%name.id` before `%name`). |
| T4 | BB-R-006.4, BB-R-005.6 default policies | `Project.defaultAccessPolicies[].profileType ∈ {Patient, Practitioner, RelatedPerson, Admin}`; `$init` seeds Patient, RelatedPerson, Admin; `defaultPatientAccessPolicy` is the legacy fallback and gates open registration. RelatedPerson default is parameterised with `%patient`. Promote/demote swap runs only on the admin member-update route, not on FHIR PUT. |
| T5 | BB-R-005.4 invite body | Current shape: `resourceType`, `firstName`, `lastName`, `email` **or** `externalId`, `patient`, `scope`, `password`, `sendEmail`, `mfaRequired`, `membership: Partial<ProjectMembership>` (wins), `upsert`, `forceNewMembership`; top-level `admin/accessPolicy/access` are deprecated but must still be accepted for v0.2. Response is **200** with the `ProjectMembership`, not 201. Email-send failure returns 200 with an error `OperationOutcome` after resources were created. |
| T6 | BB-R-005.2 scope defaults | Add: RelatedPerson defaults `server`; any invite with `externalId` is forced `project`; existing same-scope user is reused and `password/mfaRequired/names` silently ignored; cross-scope duplicate in one project → 409. |
| T7 | BB-R-005.3 membership | `user` is `User | ClientApplication | Bot`; for clients and bots `user === profile` (self-referential, no User behind them). Add `Project.owner` (owner's membership cannot be deleted) and the cascade: deleting the last membership of a **project-scoped** User deletes the User (never the profile). |
| T8 | BB-R-005.5 external IDs | Medplum keeps them on `ProjectMembership.externalId` (search param `external-id`), not on the profile `identifier`. v0.1 placement is fine; must move by v0.2 SDK-grade. |
| T9 | BB-R-005.6 settings list | `rateLimit`/quotas are `systemSetting` (super-admin only), not `setting`. Runtime-read `setting` names: `mfaRequired`, `allowedMfaMethods`, `appName`, `allowedPractitionerEmailDomain`, `googleAuthProfilePictures`, `allow-dangerous-redirect`, `autoDownload*`, `patientEverythingInlineAttachments`, `preCommitSubscriptionsEnabled`, `redactAuditEvents`. 17 feature codes listed in RES-018. |
| T10 | BB-R-004.5 "direct external auth — defer v0.2" | With Keycloak as issuer this is the baseline; reword to "third-party issuers accepted directly as bearer (Medplum `externalAuthProviders`) — defer v0.2". |
| T11 | BB-R-004.8a non-goal list | Also non-goal: `/auth/method`, `/auth/login/:id`, `/auth/google`, `/auth/external`, `/auth/exchange`, `/auth/clientinfo/:id`, `/auth/mfa/*` (all seven), `/oauth2/introspect` (Keycloak's own is advertised instead), `/oauth2/register`. `/auth/revoke` and `/auth/preauthorize` are **defer v0.2**, not non-goal. |
| T12 | BB-R-004.2 "client assertion" | Keep. But `MedplumClient.startJwtBearerLogin` (`grant_type=jwt-bearer`) is **not** a Medplum server feature; do not list it as a compat target. |
| T13 | BB-R-014.3 `User` "read-only projection" | Fine for SDK-grade. App-grade (v1.0) needs `User` writes (`mfaRequired`, names), `User/$rescope`, `User/$update-user-email`; note in the v1.0 line. `firstName`/`lastName` are `1..1`, always emit both. |
| T14 | BB-R-002.6 `_compartment` | Medplum has both `_project` (over `meta.project`) and `_compartment` (over `meta.compartment`). Emit `Project/<id>` in `meta.compartment` for extended-mode responses or the SDK's client-side compartment check always fails. |
| T15 | v0.3 placeholders | `Agent` resource type has no brick ID; give the HL7v2 agent line one (BB-R-028) so RES-002 and the six `Agent/$*` operations have a home. |
| T16 | Non-goals list | Add explicitly: Enterprise, Package/PackageRelease/PackageInstallation (marketplace), SmartHealthLink, MCP surfaces (`/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource`, `/oauth2/register`, `api-catalog`), `Project.site` (Google/reCAPTCHA per site), `ClientApplication.signInForm`, `Bot.cdsService`, `strictMode`, project-scoped issuers (`/projects/:id/oauth2`). |
| T17 | docs vs code | `user-management/index.md` shows `PATCH /admin/projects/:id/members/:mid`; the route does not exist (POST only). Do not implement PATCH for compat. |

## Area 1 — unique layer (draft)

### 1.1 Admin and super-admin routes (`/admin/projects/*`, `/admin/super/*`)


Source snapshot: medplum `packages/server/src` (admin/*.ts, app.ts, context.ts, oauth/middleware.ts, util/url.ts, auth/*.ts, fhir/accesspolicy.ts, fhir/operations/botinit.ts, fhir/operations/utils/asyncjobexecutor.ts). Docs snapshot: `docs/medplum-docs` scraped 2026-09-12. `Source` paths are relative to `packages/server/src`. BB-R mapping is against REQUIREMENTS.md at big-book commit `b4d4aad` (ADR-003 and ADR-004 decided).

Conventions used in Behaviour: "project admin" = `ctx.project.superAdmin || ctx.membership.admin`; "super admin" = `ctx.project.superAdmin`. All error bodies are FHIR `OperationOutcome`. `MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH`=8, `MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH`=72 bytes (`constants.ts:55,65`).

| ID | Capability | Source (file:line) | Behaviour | Doc ref | Used by | Proposed fate | BB-R |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ADM-001 | Admin router mount `/admin/*` | `app.ts:20,246`; `admin/routes.ts:7-9` | `apiRouter.use('/admin/', adminRouter)`; adminRouter mounts `projectAdminRouter` at `/admin/projects/` and `superAdminRouter` at `/admin/super/`. Express path matching, so `/admin/projects/setpassword` is declared before `/:projectId` routes and wins for POST. No versioning, no `/fhir/R4` prefix; JSON in/out (`Content-Type: application/json`). | `api/project-admin/invite.md — Reference > API > Invite User Endpoint` (only the sub-pages are documented) | `packages/core/src/client.ts:4623` (`medplum.invite`), app `packages/app/src/admin/*` | glue — Big Book admin API mounted at same prefix | BB-R-014.4 (v0.2), BB-R-005.4/5.5 |
| ADM-002 | Project-admin gate for all `/admin/projects/*` | `admin/project.ts:26-27`; `admin/utils.ts:13-20`; `oauth/middleware.ts:42-57`; `util/url.ts:27-31` | `authenticateRequest`: no valid bearer → 401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer realm="<baseUrl>"`. `verifyProjectAdmin`: passes if `ctx.project.superAdmin` or `ctx.membership.admin`, else 403 `forbidden`. The `:projectId` path segment is NOT compared to the token's project: `getProjectIdFromUrl` only recognises the `/projects/<id>/` URL-mount prefix (segment 1), so for `/admin/projects/<id>/...` it returns `undefined`. Every handler except invite/client therefore operates on `ctx.project` (the token's project) and ignores the path id; tests confirm a non-admin member gets 403 (`project.test.ts:167-180`). | none (implicit in every project-admin page) | all app admin pages | glue — map Keycloak org role `admin` → membership.admin claim | BB-R-005.3, BB-R-004.9 |
| ADM-003 | Super-admin gate for `/admin/super/*` | `admin/super.ts:58`; `context.ts:283-289` | Router applies `authenticateRequest` (401 as above); each handler calls `requireSuperAdmin()` which throws 403 `forbidden` unless `ctx.project.superAdmin === true`. On success the server creates an `AsyncJob` resource (`status: accepted`, `request: <full URL incl. query>`), returns 202 with `Content-Location: {baseUrl}fhir/R4/job/{id}/status` and an `accepted` OperationOutcome. `sendAsyncResponse` runs the callback in-process (fire-and-forget); `exec.run` variants instead enqueue a BullMQ worker job. | `self-hosting/upgrading-server.md — Deploy & Operate > Self-Hosting > Advanced > Upgrading Medplum Server` (`/admin/super/asyncjob` dashboard) | `packages/core/src/client.ts:3570` (`startAsyncRequest`), app `SuperAdminStartAsyncJob.tsx` | wire HAPI — HAPI `$reindex`/batch2 jobs have own status URLs; no Medplum AsyncJob | BB-R-001.7 (defer v0.2) |

#### Contradictions with REQUIREMENTS.md/ADRs

1. **BB-R-005.4 field list is the deprecated shape.** Brick: "`POST /admin/projects/:id/invite` with resourceType, name, email, `scope`, `admin`, `accessPolicy`, `mfaRequired`, `sendEmail`". Medplum's current `InviteRequest` (`packages/core/src/client.ts:612-637`) marks top-level `admin`, `accessPolicy`, `access` as `@deprecated` in favour of `membership: Partial<ProjectMembership>`; the docs page and app send `membership.admin`. It also has `firstName`/`lastName` (no `name`), `externalId`, `patient`, `password`, `upsert`, `forceNewMembership`. For v0.2 byte-compat (BB-R-005.5a / BB-R-014.4) both the deprecated and `membership.*` forms must be accepted, with `membership.*` winning (ADM-016).
2. **BB-R-005.2 scope defaults are incomplete.** Brick: "Practitioner defaults `server`, Patient defaults `project`." Code (ADM-009): RelatedPerson also defaults `server`, and any invite with `externalId` is forced project-scoped regardless of `resourceType`/`scope`.
3. **BB-R-006.4 covers only two of four default policies.** Brick: "Default policies per project (Practitioner default, Admin default) with the promote/demote swap." Medplum `Project.defaultAccessPolicies[].profileType` is `'Patient'|'Practitioner'|'RelatedPerson'|'Admin'`; invite assigns Patient (with legacy `defaultPatientAccessPolicy` fallback) and RelatedPerson (parameterised `%patient`) defaults too (ADM-017). BB-R-005.6 lists only `defaultPatientAccessPolicy`, the legacy field.
4. **BB-R-004.9 / ADR-003 assume `:projectId` is authoritative.** The brick's exit test ("`/auth/me` under each token shows the right membership") is fine, but any BB implementation that validates `:projectId` against the token will be *stricter* than Medplum, where the path id is ignored for project admins (ADM-002) and only honoured for super admins on invite/client. Not a contradiction in text, but a wire-compat trap: Medplum's app passes the id and expects it to be ignored when it equals the current project.
5. **BB-R-013.3 "Clients (create → shows secret once)".** Medplum does not show the secret once: `ClientApplication.secret` is stored in clear and returned on every FHIR read and in ADM-019 (`project.secret` is a different field, but `ClientApplication.secret` is readable via `GET /fhir/R4/ClientApplication/{id}`). Keycloak also keeps client secrets retrievable by admins, so BB can either match Medplum (retrievable) or diverge; the brick currently asserts the divergent behaviour.
6. **BB-R-005.6 settings subset lacks `allowedPractitionerEmailDomain` and `allowedMfaMethods`.** Both are read by the invite/MFA paths (ADM-011, ADM-026); with the v0.1 subset the domain check is silently absent.
7. **BB-R-004.1 "password reset/set — Keycloak realm login flows"** does not cover the *admin-initiated* variants (ADM-005, ADM-027, ADM-033) or admin MFA reset (ADM-026) that the Medplum app's Account Security panel calls; those are Keycloak admin-API operations, not login flows. Marked GAP above.
8. **BB-R-014.4 lists `/admin/projects`, `/admin/projects/:id`, `/admin/projects/:id/invite|client|members`.** There is no `GET /admin/projects` (list) route in Medplum; the app lists projects via `/auth/me` and FHIR `Project` search. Conversely `/admin/projects/:id/bot|settings|secrets|sites|setpassword` and `members/:id/mfa/reset|resetpassword` are absent from the brick.
9. **Docs vs code:** `user-management/index.md` shows `PATCH /admin/projects/:id/members/:mid` with JSON Patch; no such route exists (only POST with a full resource). The Big Book port should not implement PATCH for compat.
10. **BB-R-005.7 / ADR-004 `X-Project` header vs Medplum path id.** Brick (commit `b4d4aad`): "Super-admin tokens select the target partition with an `X-Project` header." Medplum has no such header: a super admin targets another project via the `:projectId` path segment, and only on `invite` (ADM-008, `systemRepo.readResource('Project', :projectId)`) and `client` (ADM-007); on every other project-admin route the super admin acts on its *own* project (ADM-002/019-027). For v0.2 SDK-grade compat the path id must remain authoritative for super admins on those two routes; `X-Project` can be additive, but ADR-004 #4 ("a super-admin write without it gets 400") would break `@medplum/core`/CLI callers that only send the path id. Recommend: path id wins when present; `X-Project` only for routes that have no path id (`/fhir/R4/*`).
11. **ADR-004 screen inventory names routes that do not exist in Medplum.** "`GET /admin/projects`, `GET/PUT /admin/projects/:id`, `GET /admin/projects/:id/members[?profileType=]`, `PUT /admin/projects/:id/members/:mid`" (and BB-R-013.1 "project switcher = `GET /admin/projects`"). Medplum has none of these: there is no project list route (the app uses `/auth/me` → `logins[]`/`projects` and FHIR `Project` search); project edits are `POST .../settings|secrets|sites` (ADM-020..022), not `PUT`; members are listed by FHIR search `ProjectMembership?profile-type=Practitioner|Patient|ClientApplication|Bot` (never via `/admin`); member update is `POST` (ADM-024), not `PUT`. Fine as Big Book-only routes in v0.1, but they must not be documented as Medplum-compatible and BB-R-014.4 should list the Medplum spellings from this table instead.

#### Notes for the Java port

- **`:projectId` is decorative for project admins.** Only invite (ADM-008) and client (ADM-007) read it, and only when the caller is super admin; everything else uses the token's project. `@medplum/app` always passes the current project id, so a strict implementation (403 on mismatch) is compatible with the app but stricter than Medplum. Recommend: enforce equality for non-super-admins (cheap, safer), honour the path id for super admins, and treat ADR-004's `X-Project` header as a fallback for routes without a path id (see contradiction 10).
- **Super-admin "act on another project" is inconsistent in Medplum itself.** A super admin can invite into or create a client for project X via the path id, but `GET /admin/projects/X`, settings/secrets/sites, and member routes silently operate on the super admin's own project (`ctx.project`). The Medplum app works around this by having the super admin *log in to* the target project (`/auth/me` `logins[]` switcher). Big Book's `X-Project` design (ADR-004) is cleaner; just keep the two path-id routes byte-compatible.
- **Super admin is a project property.** `superAdmin` lives on `Project`, and a token's powers depend on which project it was issued for. In Keycloak this maps to a realm role granted only to memberships of the bootstrap project (BB-R-005.7); `/auth/me` must expose `project.superAdmin: true` for `@medplum/app` to render the Super Admin menu.
- **Invite is non-atomic and ordered:** User (conditional create) → `UserSecurityRequest` → profile (conditional create) → membership → email. A failure after step 1 leaves a User with no membership; a re-invite then finds it (`existingUser=true`) and sends the "existing user" email with no set-password link. Keycloak port: create user with `UPDATE_PASSWORD` required action, then org membership, then send `execute-actions-email` last; make the whole thing idempotent on (email, project, scope).
- **Existing-user semantics:** for an existing same-scope User, `password`, `mfaRequired`, `firstName`, `lastName` are silently ignored; only a membership is added. Same for an existing profile matched by email in the project (names ignored).
- **Server- vs project-scoped Users are distinct identities** with the same email; the guard in ADM-010 blocks mixing scopes in one project unless `forceNewMembership`. Keycloak has one user per realm per email; project-scoped users can be modelled as users whose only org membership is that org (and `User.project` claim set) — the "same email, two scopes" case cannot be reproduced without a suffixed username. Decide consciously (recommend: single identity, reject cross-scope invites with 409 as Medplum does).
- **Response codes to mirror in v0.2:** invite → **200** (not 201) with `ProjectMembership`; bot and client → **201**; member update with wrong `resourceType`/`id` → **403** (not 400); RelatedPerson email matching >1 → **412** `multipleMatches`; duplicate membership → **409**; email-send failure → **200** with an `OperationOutcome` (`id: allOk`, one `error` issue) after resources were created. `@medplum/app` `InvitePage` inspects that outcome to show the "SES not configured" warning.
- **Email templates** are plain text, sender "Medplum", subjects `Welcome to Medplum` / `Medplum: Welcome to {project}` / `Medplum Password Reset` / `Your multi-factor authentication has been reset`. Links: `{appBaseUrl}setpassword/{id}/{secret}` and `{appBaseUrl}signin`. `sendEmail` must be boolean `false` to suppress (the docs' `"sendEmail":"false"` string still sends). Per-project SMTP/app-name comes from `Project.setting` (v0.2, BB-R-026).
- **`UserSecurityRequest` semantics:** one-time secret (16 bytes hex), typed (`invite`/`reset`/`verify-email`), superseding prior unused requests of the same type, expiry by type, consumed in a transaction. Keycloak action tokens cover this; BB should not persist its own.
- **`setPassword` side effects:** HIBP breach check (outbound HTTPS to api.pwnedpasswords.com — must be optional/offline-safe in `lite`), then revoke all `Login`s. Keycloak: `logout` all user sessions after admin password set to match.
- **MFA reset rotates the TOTP secret** and un-enrols only the chosen method; Medplum stores `mfaSecret` on the User even before enrolment (`mfaRequired` pre-generates it). Keycloak equivalent: delete the `otp` credential and add `CONFIGURE_TOTP` required action; "email" MFA has no Keycloak analogue in v0.x.
- **Membership delete cascade:** deleting the last membership of a project-scoped User deletes the User (never the Practitioner/Patient profile); `Project.owner` cannot be removed. BB needs an owner concept (or accept the divergence) and must delete the Keycloak user only when it is org-only.
- **Promote/demote swap** (`reconcileDefaultAccessPolicy`) runs only on ADM-024, not on plain FHIR `PUT ProjectMembership`; it swaps only when the current policy is exactly the *other* default. If BB implements membership update through the FHIR route as well, apply the same reconcile there or document the difference.
- **Settings/secrets/sites are whole-array replaces** of `Project.setting|secret|site` producing a new Project version; secrets are plaintext in the resource and echoed back by ADM-019. BB should store secrets outside the Project resource (Keycloak client attributes/Vault) and return names only; `@medplum/app` `SecretsPage` expects `project.secret[]` values to edit them, so app-grade compat (v1.0) would need masked values or a divergence note.
- **ClientApplication creation** happens through the *system* repo, so a project admin whose access policy forbids `ClientApplication` writes can still create clients through this route; the secret is 64 hex chars and remains readable. Keycloak: create confidential client with `bigbook.project` attribute + service-account user, and mint the membership with `profile = ClientApplication/<id>` for `/auth/me`.
- **Bot creation** creates a `Binary` (source) + `Bot` + membership and optionally deploys; `runtimeVersion` defaults from server config. Under BB-R-016 bots are compiled Camel routes, so the v0.2 route should accept the same body but reject `sourceCode`/`executableCode` uploads (or store them inert) — decide in the Bot SDK ADR.
- **Super-admin DB routes (ADM-036..044) embed Postgres identifiers into DDL** (validated by `isValidPostgresIdentifier`) and run on the writer pool; `tablesettings` is synchronous. None have a HAPI/Keycloak analogue; document `psql` runbooks instead (BB-R-011.7). `reindex` maps to HAPI `$reindex` but with a different parameter set; the AsyncJob/`job/{id}/status` polling shape used by `SuperAdminStartAsyncJob` is Medplum-specific and only matters for app-grade compat (v1.0).
- **Callers outside the app worth knowing:** `packages/cli/src/utils.ts:107` (bot create) and `packages/cli/src/aws/update-server.ts:46` (migrate) call these routes; examples call bot/client/invite only. `@medplum/core` exposes exactly one typed helper (`invite`); everything else is `medplum.post('admin/...')`, so SDK-grade compat (v0.2) needs only the invite route byte-exact, while bot/client/settings are app-grade.

---

### 1.2 Custom resource types (24) and Medplum `meta` fields


Sources (all read-only, nothing modified):
- `medplum/packages/definitions/src/fhir/r4/profiles-medplum.json` — 28 entries: 24 custom `kind=resource` StructureDefinitions listed below, plus `IdentityProvider` (complex-type, used by `ClientApplication.identityProvider` and `DomainConfiguration.identityProvider`), `ViewDefinition` (logical model, SQL-on-FHIR), and 2 OperationDefinitions (`rebuild-base-definitions`, `Translate`). Parsed with python3; element tables below are exhaustive for the seven Part B types.
- `medplum/packages/definitions/src/fhir/r4/profiles-types.json` — Medplum's patched `Meta` (adds `project`, `author`, `onBehalfOf`, `account`, `accounts`, `compartment`, `deleted`).
- `medplum/packages/definitions/src/fhir/r4/search-parameters-medplum.json`, `valuesets-medplum.json` (feature codes, bound value sets).
- `medplum/packages/core/src/access.ts` (`protectedResourceTypes`, `projectAdminResourceTypes`), `packages/server/src/fhir/{repo,accesspolicy,sharding}.ts`, `admin/{invite,client,project}.ts`, `auth/me.ts`, `oauth/{token,utils,clients}.ts`, `bots/*`, `fhir/operations/*`.
- Docs: paths below are relative to `big-book-of-boo-boos/docs/medplum-docs/`; the quoted string is the `menu:` breadcrumb from the page frontmatter.
- "Used by": example folder names under `medplum/examples/` that mention the type in .ts/.tsx/.json, and files under `medplum/packages/app/src` (test files excluded).

Note: there is **no `publicResourceTypes`** constant in this snapshot of `@medplum/core`. The old "public project" concept is replaced by the synthetic **FHIR R4 project** (`r4ProjectId = 161452d9-43b7-5c29-aa7b-c85680fa45c6`, `server/src/constants.ts`), which is implicitly linked into every repository with `exportedResourceType = [StructureDefinition, ValueSet, CodeSystem, SearchParameter, OperationDefinition]`. Base definitions live there and are read-only for everyone.

Access tiers used in the tables (from `core/src/access.ts` + `server/src/fhir/accesspolicy.ts#applyProjectAdminAccessPolicy`):
- **protected** = `['DomainConfiguration','Enterprise','JsonWebKey','Login']` — `meta.project` is left undefined, `supportsInteraction`/`canPerformInteraction` return false for everyone except a super-admin project; excluded from `$export`.
- **project-admin** = `['Cron','Package','PackageRelease','PackageInstallation','Project','ProjectMembership','User','UserSecurityRequest']` — a `resourceType: '*'` policy entry never matches them; non-admin memberships get every entry for these types stripped; `membership.admin` gets a fixed injected rule set (below); a `Project.superAdmin` project gets unrestricted entries. Cross-project reads through `Project.link` never cross for these types except `Project` itself.
- **ordinary** = every other custom type (AccessPolicy, ClientApplication, Bot, UserConfiguration, Agent, AsyncJob, BulkDataExport, Dicom*, SmartAppLaunch, SmartHealthLink) — governed only by the caller's compiled AccessPolicy like any FHIR resource.

Injected rules for `membership.admin` (verbatim from `applyProjectAdminAccessPolicy`): own `Project` (`Project?_id=<own>`, interactions all except delete, `readonlyFields: [features, link, systemSetting]`, `hiddenFields: [superAdmin, systemSecret, strictMode]`); linked Projects read-only with `hiddenFields: [superAdmin, setting, systemSetting, secret, systemSecret, strictMode]`; `ProjectMembership?_project=<own>` with `readonlyFields: [project, user]`; `UserSecurityRequest?_project=<own>` readonly; `User?_project=<own>` with `hiddenFields: [passwordHash, mfaSecret]`, `readonlyFields: [email, emailVerified, mfaEnrolled, project]`; `Cron?_project=<own>`; `Package`/`PackageRelease`/`PackageInstallation` readonly.

| ID | Resource type | Purpose | Who may read/write | Key elements | Server-side behaviour | Doc ref | Used by | Proposed fate | BB-R |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RES-001 | AccessPolicy | Declarative per-resource-type rules (criteria, interactions, hidden/read-only fields, write constraints, IP rules) that a ProjectMembership points at. | ordinary; effectively project admins create them; anyone whose policy grants `AccessPolicy` can read. | `name:string[0..1]`, `basedOn:Reference<AccessPolicy>[0..*]`, `compartment:Reference[0..1]`, `resource[0..*]{resourceType:string[1..1], criteria:string, interaction:code[0..*], readonly:boolean(dep), hiddenFields:string[0..*], readonlyFields:string[0..*], writeConstraint:Expression[0..*], compartment:Reference(dep)}`, `ipAccessRule[0..*]{name, value:string[1..1], action:code(allow|block)[1..1]}` | Nothing special on its own CRUD (validated only). Compiled per request in `getRepoForLogin` → `buildAccessPolicy(membership)`: reads `membership.accessPolicy` + every `membership.access[].policy`, JSON-string-substitutes `%<param>` / `%<param>.id` (auto-adds `%profile`, and `%patient` = profile unless supplied), maps `readonly:true` → `interaction:[search,read,history,vread]`, concatenates all `resource[]` entries (OR semantics), merges all `ipAccessRule`, adds read-only `SearchParameter`/`StructureDefinition` entries, then `applyProjectAdminAccessPolicy` (see tiers). No policy at all ⇒ `{resourceType:'*'}` full access. ##### Medplum-specific `meta` fields (from the patched `Meta` type in profiles-types.json)

All are **stripped from every read/search response unless the request carries `X-Medplum: extended`** (`removeHiddenFields`); `@medplum/core` `MedplumClient` sends that header by default (`extendedMode !== false`), and `@medplum/core` `bundle.ts` deletes `meta.author/compartment/project` when building transaction bundles.

| ID | Element | Type/Card | Server behaviour | Doc ref | Proposed fate | BB-R |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RES-M01 | `meta.project` | uri [0..1] | Server-assigned on every write (`getProjectId`): Project → own id; ProjectMembership → its `project`; protected types → undefined; User/Subscription → caller-supplied for super-admin (server-scoped when absent); any type → caller-supplied only when super-admin (`canWriteProtectedMeta`), else existing value or the context's current project. Backs the `projectId` column, the `_project` search param (`core/types.ts` synthesises `<Type>.meta.project`), `_compartment=Project/<id>`, and every read check (`canPerformInteraction` requires `meta.project ∈ permittedProjectIds`). | `access/projects.md` "Security & Compliance > Authorization and Access Control > Projects" | **glue** — map to HAPI partition id on read/write interceptor | BB-R-005.1, BB-R-002.6 |
| RES-M02 | `meta.author` | Reference [0..1] | Always `context.author` (= membership.profile, or system for admin routes); **never taken from the request body**. Used by Subscription search param `author`. | `access/projects.md` (mentions) | **glue** — emit from token `profile` claim in response interceptor; persist in HAPI as Provenance or an extension | GAP |
| RES-M03 | `meta.onBehalfOf` | Reference [0..1] | Set from `X-Medplum-On-Behalf-Of` header resolution (`context.onBehalfOf`), server-controlled. | none | **defer v0.2** — with On-Behalf-Of | BB-R-004.6, BB-R-024 |
| RES-M04 | `meta.account` (deprecated) / `meta.accounts` | Reference [0..1] / [0..*] | `getAccounts`: caller-supplied only when `extendedMode && (superAdmin || projectAdmin)`; otherwise on create = creator's `AccessPolicy.compartment`, plus for non-Patient resources the accounts of every Patient in the resource's patient compartment (propagation); `$set-accounts` async op rewrites them. Feed `meta.compartment`. `core/utils.ts#getAccounts` normalises both. | `access/access-policies.md` (compartments) | **defer v0.2** — sub-project compartments; not in v0.1 bricks | GAP |
| RES-M05 | `meta.compartment` | Reference [0..*] | Computed on every write (`getCompartments`): `Project/<meta.project>`, `User.project`, each non-Project account, each Patient reference from the R4 Patient CompartmentDefinition. Backs `_compartment` search and the deprecated `AccessPolicy.resource.compartment` check (`core/access.ts` checks it client-side too). | `access/access-policies.md` | **glue** — emit `Project/<partition>` + HAPI patient compartment on read | BB-R-002.6 |
| RES-M06 | `meta.deleted` | boolean [0..1] | Tombstone marker on history versions produced by delete. | none | **wire HAPI** — HAPI history returns 410 entries | BB-R-001.1/3 |
| RES-M07 | `meta.lastUpdated` / `meta.versionId` / `id` (protected writes) | — | `lastUpdated` honoured from body only for super-admin (`canWriteProtectedMeta`); `id` on create honoured only for super-admin (`canSetId`); otherwise update-as-create → 404/400. | `fhir-datastore/resource-history.md` "Build > FHIR Datastore > FHIR Data > Resource History" | **wire HAPI** — server-assigned ids, update-as-create off | BB-R-001.9, BB-R-005.7 |

---

#### PART B — one row per element (Project, ProjectMembership, AccessPolicy, ClientApplication, User, UserConfiguration, Bot)

Every type also carries the standard DomainResource elements `id`, `meta`, `implicitRules`, `language`, `text`, `contained`, `extension`, `modifierExtension` (all `0..1`/`0..*`, standard meaning); they are listed once per type as a single row. `meta` behaviour is in RES-M01…M07. Fate vocabulary as in Part A; `data only` = stored/echoed, no server logic.

##### Project

| Type.element | Type | Card | Meaning | Behaviour when set | Fate |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Project.{id,meta,implicitRules,language,text,contained,extension,modifierExtension} | base | — | Standard DomainResource elements. `meta.project` = own id. | `id` server-assigned; `meta.project` self-referential | glue |
| Project.identifier | Identifier | 0..* | External identifiers for the project. | search param `identifier`; data only | glue (store) |
| Project.name | string | 0..1 | Display name. | returned by `/auth/me`, `/admin/projects/:id`; search `name` | glue |
| Project.description | string | 0..1 | Free text. | returned by `/auth/me` | glue |
| Project.superAdmin | boolean | 0..1 | Marks the super-admin project; members get full server access. | `Repository.superAdmin`: bypasses policy, may read protected types, set `id`/`meta.project`/`lastUpdated`, see all projects; hidden from project admins; `applyProjectAdminAccessPolicy` adds all admin types | glue — Keycloak realm role `super-admin` |
| Project.strictMode | boolean | 0..1 | Deprecated. Strict StructureDefinition validation vs legacy JSON-schema validation. | `validateRepositoryResource`: strict → profile validation errors throw; false → JSON-schema only, strict failures logged. `$init` sets true. Hidden from project admins, settable by super-admin only | non-goal — HAPI always validates per config |
| Project.checkReferencesOnWrite | boolean | 0..1 | Referential integrity on create/update. | `Repository.checkReferencesOnWrite` → every reference must resolve and be readable, else 400 | wire HAPI (`enforce_referential_integrity_on_write`) |
| Project.owner | Reference<User> | 0..1 | Owning user. | `$init` sets; `DELETE …/members/:id` refuses owner's membership; owner must not be a project-scoped User of another project; search `owner` | glue (data + delete guard) |
| Project.features | code[] | 0..* | Feature flags (value set `project-feature`). Codes: `ai`, `ai-realtime`, `aws-comprehend`, `aws-textract`, `billing`, `bots`, `cron`, `email`, `google-auth-required`, `graphql-introspection`, `scheduling`, `websocket-subscriptions`, `transaction-bundles`, `validate-terminology`, `range-search`, `log-streaming`, `async-batch`; docs also list `terminology`. | Server reads: `bots` (bot create/execute/deploy), `cron` (Cron worker), `email` (`/email/send`), `google-auth-required` (reject non-Google logins), `graphql-introspection`, `websocket-subscriptions`, `transaction-bundles` (real DB transactions), `validate-terminology` (binding validation), `range-search`, `async-batch`, `aws-textract`, `aws-comprehend`, `ai`, `ai-realtime`. `readonlyFields` for project admins; returned by `/auth/me`; search `features` | glue — store list; honour `bots`, `cron`, `graphql-introspection`, `transaction-bundles` only |
| Project.defaultPatientAccessPolicy | Reference<AccessPolicy> | 0..1 | Deprecated; policy for Patient invites and open registration. | Invite fallback when `defaultAccessPolicies[Patient]` absent; `/auth/newpatient` and SCIM patient create require it | glue (v0.1 data; v0.2 open registration) |
| Project.setting | BackboneElement[] | 0..* | Key/value visible to all members, writable by project admins. | Names read by server: `mfaRequired`, `allowedMfaMethods`, `appName`, `googleAuthProfilePictures`, `allow-dangerous-redirect`, `autoDownloadEnabled`, `autoDownloadAllowedUrlPrefixes`, `autoDownloadIgnoredUrlPrefixes`, `patientEverythingInlineAttachments`, `preCommitSubscriptionsEnabled`, `redactAuditEvents`. Hidden from admins of *linked* projects. `POST /admin/projects/:id/settings` replaces array | glue — store; honour `mfaRequired`, `appName` |
| Project.setting.name | string | 1..1 | Setting key. | as above | glue |
| Project.setting.value[x] | string \| boolean \| decimal \| integer | 1..1 | Setting value. | as above | glue |

| Project.secret.name / value[x] | string / string\|boolean\|decimal\|integer | 1..1 | as setting | as above | glue |

| Project.systemSetting.name / value[x] | string / choice | 1..1 | as setting | as above | defer v0.2 |

| Project.systemSecret.name / value[x] | string / choice | 1..1 | as setting | as above | defer v0.2 |

---

#### Contradictions with REQUIREMENTS.md / ADRs

1. **BB-R-006.2 — "`admin: true` bypasses."** Not true in Medplum. `membership.admin` never bypasses the compiled AccessPolicy; `accesspolicy.ts` says so explicitly ("The `admin` flag does not bypass the access policy") and `applyProjectAdminAccessPolicy` only *adds* a fixed rule set for the project-admin resource types. Only `Project.superAdmin` bypasses (`Repository.isSuperAdmin()` short-circuits `canPerformInteraction`). What makes admins look unrestricted is that a membership with **no** `accessPolicy`/`access` compiles to `{resourceType:'*'}`. Big Book should model: super-admin project = bypass; admin = ordinary policy + injected admin rules; no policy = full access within project.

2. **BB-R-006.2 — "`ProjectMembership.accessPolicy[]` … list".** `accessPolicy` is `0..1`. The list is `ProjectMembership.access[]` (`{policy, parameter[]}`), and both are concatenated then OR'd. The exit test in BB-R-006 (single policy) still holds; the schema statement does not.

3. **BB-R-006.3 — "`%requestor`".** No `%requestor` variable exists in server code; the built-ins are `%profile` and `%patient` (the latter defaults to the profile unless a `patient` parameter is passed). Everything else comes from `access[].parameter`. Substitution is a raw JSON-string `replaceAll`, so `%name.id` must be substituted before `%name`.

4. **BB-R-006.4 — "Default policies per project (Practitioner default, Admin default)".** Medplum's `Project.defaultAccessPolicies[].profileType` is bound to `Patient | Practitioner | RelatedPerson | Admin`, and `$init` seeds **Patient, RelatedPerson, Admin** (not Practitioner). The promote/demote swap only fires when a membership's policy is *exactly* one of the two recognised defaults. The brick should name all four codes.

5. **BB-R-005.2 — scope defaults.** Matches code, but code adds a third rule the brick omits: **any invite carrying `externalId` is forced project-scoped regardless of resourceType**. Also `User.firstName/lastName` are `1..1` — a "read-only projection of Keycloak" (BB-R-014.3) must always emit both or `@medplum/fhirtypes`-typed consumers break.

6. **BB-R-005.3 — "ProjectMembership links User → Project".** `ProjectMembership.user` is `Reference<User | ClientApplication | Bot>`, and for clients and bots **`user` and `profile` are the same resource**. There is no `User` behind a ClientApplication or Bot. Keycloak-only identity for clients is fine, but the membership row still needs a `user` reference of type `ClientApplication`.

7. **BB-R-005.5 — "External IDs on identities — v0.1 as identifier on the profile resource".** Medplum carries external ids on `ProjectMembership.externalId` (and deprecated `User.externalId`), with a dedicated `external-id` search parameter and a `%`-free lookup in external auth. Putting them on the profile `identifier` diverges from what `MedplumClient`/app users search for; acceptable in v0.1 but must move for v0.2 SDK-grade C.

8. **BB-R-005.6 — settings subset says `rateLimit` is a project setting.** In Medplum `rateLimit`/`authRateLimit`/`loginRateLimit`/quotas are **`systemSetting`** (super-admin only), not `setting`. Conversely `mfaRequired` (BB-R-004.3) is a `Project.setting`, not an invite-only flag: `isMfaRequired = Project.setting[mfaRequired] || User.mfaRequired`.

9. **BB-R-005.7 — "only role that can … override `id`/`meta`".** Correct, with one nuance: a project admin in extended mode may set `meta.account(s)` (`canWriteAccount`), and super-admins may set `meta.project` only for `User` and `Subscription` unless they also hold `canWriteProtectedMeta` (which they do). Effectively matches.

10. **BB-R-005.11 — `X-Project` header for super-admin tokens.** Medplum has no such header. A super-admin token acts in the super-admin project; cross-project writes are done by setting `meta.project` in the body (extended mode), and cross-project reads by searching with `_project=` / `_compartment=`. Introducing `X-Project` is a Big Book invention; it must not be required by `@medplum/core` paths (the SDK will never send it) or SDK-grade C breaks for super-admin users. Recommend: accept `X-Project` **and** `meta.project`, never require either for reads.

11. **BB-R-014.3 — "`User` (read-only projection of Keycloak)".** Medplum project admins *write* Users (name, `mfaRequired`), and the app calls `User/$rescope`, `User/$update-user-email`, MFA endpoints. Read-only is fine for SDK-grade C (core never writes User), but app-grade C (v1.0) will need writes or stubbed operations.

12. **BB-R-014.3 — ClientApplication "readable/writable in Medplum JSON shape".** In Medplum the `secret` is returned on every read of the ClientApplication (no hidden field). Keycloak also allows reading a confidential client's secret via admin API, so this is reproducible, but BB-R-013.3 "Clients (create → shows secret once)" contradicts Medplum semantics; `@medplum/app` `ClientsPage` reads the secret from the resource.

13. **ADR-003 SDK-grade C exit test** lists client-credentials login, `/auth/me`, Patient CRUD/search. Two further `@medplum/core` dependencies on custom types are not in that list but are on the default code path: (a) `MedplumClient` sends `X-Medplum: extended` on every request and `getProfile()`/`getProject()`/`getUserConfiguration()`/`getAccessPolicy()` are populated from `/auth/me`; the `/auth/me` payload must include `project.{id,name,features,strictMode,superAdmin}`, `membership.{id,user,profile,admin}`, `profile`, `config` (a full `UserConfiguration` with a non-empty `menu`), `accessPolicy` (compiled, `basedOn` filled), and optionally `security`; (b) `startAsyncRequest` expects `AsyncJob` JSON at `Content-Location` for any `Prefer: respond-async` call. Neither is in BB-R-014 §3.

14. **BB-R-004.8a / ADR-003 — `Login` non-goal.** Consistent, but note `Login` is also what `/auth/me.security.sessions` and `/auth/revoke` enumerate; SDK-grade C never touches them, app-grade C does (SecurityPage).

15. **BB-R-002.6 — `_compartment=Project/<id>`.** Medplum's `_compartment` search param is synthesised over `meta.compartment`, and a separate `_project` param over `meta.project` (`core/types.ts`). `@medplum/core`'s `AccessPolicy` client-side check (`core/access.ts`) reads `resource.meta.compartment` — so if Big Book never emits `meta.compartment`, a deprecated `resource.compartment` policy evaluated in the browser always fails. Emit `Project/<id>` in `meta.compartment` for extended-mode responses.

16. **BB-R-001.5 — `Project.defaultProfile` "auto-applied when absent".** Matches Medplum exactly (only when `meta.profile` is empty for that resource type).

17. **Non-goals list says "DICOM … CDS Hooks"** — consistent with Dicom* and `Bot.cdsService` fates above. But `Agent` is not in the non-goals; it is the v0.3 HL7v2 agent config, so it needs a v0.3 brick ID (currently GAP).

#### Notes for the Java port

**Where the 24 types live on HAPI.**
- *Load as custom resources* (HAPI `IResourceProvider` + `@ResourceDef` Java classes generated from `profiles-medplum.json`, `kind=resource`, base `DomainResource`): `AccessPolicy`, `ClientApplication`, `UserConfiguration`, `Bot`, `Project`, `ProjectMembership`, `User`. These must round-trip byte-for-byte because `@medplum/core` and `@medplum/app` read/write them through `/fhir/R4/<Type>` (`medplum.readResource('Project', id)`, `searchResources('ProjectMembership', …)`, `updateResource(accessPolicy)`), and `MedplumClient` validates responses only loosely, so the risk is *shape*, not schema. Search parameters must be registered from `search-parameters-medplum.json` (e.g. `ProjectMembership?project=…&user=…&profile-type=Practitioner`, `User?email=`, `AccessPolicy?name=`, `ClientApplication?name=`), otherwise the app's admin pages return empty lists. HAPI's JSON parser rejects unknown top-level elements only in strict mode; keep it lenient and generate the Java model rather than hand-writing.
- *Stored outside FHIR, projected on demand*: `User` (Keycloak user), `ClientApplication` (Keycloak client + BB attributes), `Project` core identity (Keycloak organisation) — but their **FHIR JSON view** must still be served at `/fhir/R4/User/<id>` etc. for SDK-grade C. A small "virtual resource provider" that materialises the JSON from Keycloak (read) and writes back a whitelist of fields is cheaper than double-storing. `ProjectMembership`, `AccessPolicy`, `UserConfiguration` have no Keycloak counterpart and should be real HAPI rows in the tenant partition (`ProjectMembership` additionally indexed globally, as Medplum does via the global shard).
- *Do not model*: `Login`, `JsonWebKey`, `DomainConfiguration`, `UserSecurityRequest` (Keycloak sessions/keys/orgs/action-tokens), `Enterprise`, `Package*`, `Dicom*`, `SmartHealthLink`. `AsyncJob`/`BulkDataExport`: only needed when `Prefer: respond-async` is honoured; HAPI's bulk export uses `$export-poll-status` with a different JSON, so a thin adapter that returns an `AsyncJob`-shaped body from `Content-Location` is the v0.2 bridge. `SmartAppLaunch`, `Cron`, `Agent` per fate above.

**What must stay byte-compatible for `@medplum/core` (ADR-003 SDK-grade C).**
1. `/auth/me` response shape (see contradiction 13). `config.menu` must be non-empty (core/app build the sidebar from it); `accessPolicy` should be the *compiled* policy (`resource[]` present, `basedOn[]` = source refs) because `core/access.ts` uses it client-side for optimistic permission checks (`canWriteResource`, `satisfiedAccessPolicy`) and the app hides buttons based on it.
2. Extended meta: `X-Medplum: extended` ⇒ responses carry `meta.project` (partition id as string), `meta.author` (`{reference:'Practitioner/…'}`), `meta.compartment` (at least `Project/<id>`). Without the header, strip all Medplum meta fields. HAPI has no slot for these; implement as a response interceptor (`SERVER_OUTGOING_RESPONSE`) that injects them from the partition id and the token's `profile` claim, and a request interceptor that strips them on inbound writes (Medplum ignores client-supplied `meta.author` and, for non-super-admins, `meta.project`).
3. Project-admin semantics: `*` policy entries must not grant `Project`/`ProjectMembership`/`User`/`Cron`/`Package*`/`UserSecurityRequest`; admins get the injected rules verbatim (hidden `superAdmin`, `systemSecret`, `strictMode` on Project; hidden `passwordHash`, `mfaSecret` on User; readonly `project`,`user` on ProjectMembership). Reproduce the `readonlyFields`/`hiddenFields` restore-on-write behaviour, or the app's "edit membership" page will silently clear `project`/`user`.
4. `ProjectMembership.meta.project` is always the membership's project; `Project.meta.project` is its own id; server-scoped `User` has **no** `meta.project` — the app's Super Admin "Users" page relies on `User?_project:missing=true`-style queries for server-scoped users. In HAPI terms, server-scoped users live in the default partition.
5. Ids are UUIDs and `ClientApplication.id` **is** the OAuth `client_id`; the SDK's `startClientLogin(clientId, clientSecret)` posts `client_id=<ClientApplication.id>`. Keycloak client `clientId` must therefore equal the FHIR resource id (or Big Book maps `ClientApplication.id` ↔ Keycloak internal id, exposing the FHIR id as `client_id` via a Keycloak client attribute).
6. Value sets: `client-application-status`, `bot-runtime-version`, `project-feature`, `project-membership-profile-type`, `user-mfa-method`, `ip-access-rule-action`, `access-poliicy-interactions` (sic, typo is upstream) are in `valuesets-medplum.json`; load them into HAPI terminology so `$validate` on these types passes.

**Surprises worth recording.**
- The `*` wildcard does not cover project-admin types, and `Project.link` reads never cross for them — a "multi-tenant admin" policy must name `Project`/`ProjectMembership` explicitly.
- `ProjectMembership` for a Bot or ClientApplication is self-referential (`user === profile`); invite for Practitioners creates a *server-scoped* User by default, so "one project ↔ one Keycloak organisation" (BB-R-005.1) needs Keycloak users that belong to several organisations — Keycloak Organizations support that, but per-org attributes (`admin`, `accessPolicy`) do not exist in Keycloak and must live in Big Book's membership row.
- Parameter substitution is textual on the policy JSON, so a `%patient` placeholder inside `criteria` strings, `compartment.reference`, and `writeConstraint.expression` are all rewritten in one pass; port must do the same (or restrict to `criteria`).
- `ClientApplication.secret` is stored and returned in clear; `retiringSecret` doubles it. Both are compared with timing-safe equality.
- Login for `client_credentials` never hits Postgres (Redis only); token introspection works from the JWT + Redis. Keycloak sessions cover this natively.
- `strictMode:false` is a deliberate escape hatch that downgrades validation to JSON-schema — HAPI has no equivalent; treat as always strict.
- The synthetic FHIR R4 project (`161452d9-43b7-5c29-aa7b-c85680fa45c6`) is appended to every repository's project list; base `StructureDefinition`/`ValueSet`/`CodeSystem`/`SearchParameter`/`OperationDefinition` resolve from it and are read-only for everyone except super-admin. HAPI keeps these in its own tables (not partitioned), which is the same effect.
- `Project.features` gates whole subsystems; `defaultProjectFeatures` is server config. Big Book should seed `features` at project create from config to keep `/auth/me.project.features` meaningful to the app (it hides Bots/Cron tabs when absent).
- `UserConfiguration.option` is keyed by `Element.id` (`option[].id`), not a `name` element — an easy port mistake.
- `Cron` is validated on write (cron string + readability of target and `onBehalfOf`) — one of only two types with type-specific write validation (the other being reference checks); everything else relies on the generic validator.

---

### 1.3 Authentication and OAuth (`/auth/*`, `/oauth2/*`, `/.well-known/*`, middleware)


Source snapshot: `medplum/packages/server/src/{auth,oauth}/*.ts`, `wellknown.ts`, `ratelimit.ts`, `context.ts`; SDK `packages/core/src/client.ts`; UI `packages/app/src`, `packages/react/src/auth`; `examples/*`. All paths below are relative to `/Users/tanvirhossain/Documents/projects/github/medplum/` unless prefixed with `docs/` (which is `/Users/tanvirhossain/Documents/projects/github/big-book-of-boo-boos/docs/medplum-docs/`).

Routers are mounted in `packages/server/src/app.ts:245-257`: `/.well-known/` → `wellKnownRouter`, `/auth/` → `authRouter`, `/oauth2/` → `oauthRouter`; every router is also mounted under `/api/...` and `/projects/:projectId/...` prefixes (`util/url.ts:27-68`, see OA-020). `attachRequestContext` (`context.ts:153-197`) runs `authenticateTokenImpl` on *every* request before routing, so "authenticated" below means the request carried a valid `Authorization: Bearer` or `Authorization: Basic` header (MW-001/MW-002); routes marked `authenticateRequest` reject 401 otherwise (MW-005).

Legend for "Proposed fate": `wire Keycloak (<feature>)`, `glue`, `defer v0.2`, `defer v0.3`, `non-goal (<reason>)`. ADR-003 option B in v0.1 → only `/oauth2/authorize|token|userinfo|logout`, `/.well-known/*`, `/auth/me` exist; `Login` and `/auth/login|newuser|…` are non-goal through v0.x (BB-R-004 §8a).

#### Routes and behaviours

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AUTH-001 | `POST /auth/login` — email/password login, creates `Login` | `packages/server/src/auth/login.ts:13-60`; `oauth/utils.ts:146-221` (`tryLogin`), `auth/utils.ts:317-388` (`sendLoginResult`) | Unauthenticated JSON body: `email` (string, required, valid email), `password` (string, required, 8–72 bytes), optional `clientId`, `projectId` (UUID or literal `"new"`), `resourceType` (filters memberships to that profile type), `scope` (default `"openid"`), `nonce` (default random UUID), `launch` (SmartAppLaunch id), `codeChallenge`/`codeChallengeMethod` (`plain`/`S256`; required unless `ClientApplication.pkceOptional`), `remember` (deprecated → refresh token), `recaptcha*` ignored here. If `clientId` given, project is taken from the client (`auth/utils.ts:433-448`; mismatch → 400 `Invalid projectId`); `Origin` header must be in `ClientApplication.allowedOrigin` if set (`oauth/utils.ts:151-156`). Looks up `User` by email (project-scoped first, then server-scoped; `oauth/utils.ts:741-802`), bcrypt-compares `User.passwordHash` (400 `Email or password is invalid`; unknown user → 400 `User not found`), creates `Login{authMethod:'password', code, cookie, refreshSecret?, scope, nonce, codeChallenge, project?, profileType?, remoteAddress, userAgent}`; if exactly one active `ProjectMembership` (or `projectId==='new'`) it calls `setLoginMembership` immediately. Response (200) is one of: `{login, mfaEnrollRequired:true, enrollUri, enrollQrCode, allowedMfaMethods}`; `{login, mfaRequired:true, mfaMethods, email}`; `{login}` (project `new`); `{login, code}` (+ `Set-Cookie: medplum-<clientId>` when a client is involved, MW-011); or `{login, memberships:[{id, project:{reference,display}, profile, identifier}]}` when the user must pick a profile. Errors are FHIR `OperationOutcome` (400). Membership lookup: (A) `fhirUser` claim (or `ext.fhirUser`) as reference or search string → profile → `ProjectMembership?profile=`; (B) `sub` → `ProjectMembership?external-id=` (must be unique); (C) neither + project URL → the client's own membership. Creates a real `Login{authMethod:'external', granted: undefined}` and logs an AuditEvent; the resulting Login is cached in Redis (`medplum:ext-auth:<iss>:<project>:<sha256(token)>`, 1 h).
